# Ideas and what the Arena said

Every strategy idea we tested during the Arena (7-8 Nov 2026), on its own simulator and tapes, with its date, data, command and numbers. The ideas tested before the event on IMC Prosperity data are in the prep repository.

<!-- ideas-index:start -->

### Not yet tested (0)

<!-- ideas-index:end -->

**A result here is a prior about the Arena, not a verdict.** The Arena's products, bots, matching and scoring will differ from Prosperity's. So an idea that loses here stays on this list, marked `dropped here`, and is tested again on the Arena's data. An idea that wins here is tested again too. `ON_THE_DAY.md` ("Every round") points here.

**Keeping it up to date:**
- When a test runs, add the result to its row: the date, the data, the command and the numbers. Keep the earlier results and add the new one under them.
- After any edit to a row, `uv run python -m experiments.ideas_index` rewrites the index at the top (`python/tests/test_ideas_index.py` fails until it is current).
- Each verdict is one of these:
  - `kept`: it is in the template;
  - `dropped here`: it is not in the template, and is re-tested on the day anyway;
  - `measured`: a fact about the data, not a strategy;
  - `open`: tested, with no conclusion yet.
- **`--resting` results before 2026-10-13's review fixes are a little high.** Until then a resting order crossed by the next snapshot could also fill from that tick's market trades at the same price, which used the same orders twice. The fix took 0.3–3.6% off the template alone (P3 round 1: 68,043 to 67,446) and 13–16% off its runs against copies of itself. The rows below that were re-run say so; older `--resting` numbers elsewhere were not re-run.
- On the day, for each round, go down the "Re-test on the Arena" column and run the rows whose condition fits that round's products, again in every round: the rounds get harder, so an Arena result from an earlier round is a prior too.

**What a market we didn't write said about these priors** (2026-10-27; ABIDES's RMSC04, 10 days, "An independent generator: ABIDES's RMSC04" and the rows after it; LOBSTER's real book is still to come, the prep repository's `MANUAL_TODO.md`):
- **Held:** the statistics and the tools. The grid's pick rule, the held-out checks and StepM named the settings that also won on held-out ABIDES days; `diagnose`'s notes read the market correctly (large-tick, trades move the wall mid, a thin margin); the fill-model spread (`all` against `worse` against resting) mattered as much as there.
- **Did not hold:** the playbook. Prosperity's one wall at the value, its uninformed flow and its free inventory are what make plain market making pay. In an agent market with informed and momentum traders, no fair mode finds the value (all 34 ticks off), passive fills lose to adverse selection, moves continue, and every quoting setting of the template as committed loses with resting orders (doing nothing beats it). The shuffle test tells the two apart in one command: shuffling Prosperity's ticks changes nothing, shuffling ABIDES's turns a loss into millions.
- **What paid there instead:** a fair that leans on the flow and the trend (`lin_rho3` 3, `lin_momentum` 0.5 over 100 ticks: +1.1M to +1.2M under every replay mode, and +1.27M inside the market itself, where the template as committed loses 230k; re-run 2026-10-28, the fourth audit's R13 and R14), or pulling the side a move runs over (`gate_move` 2 over 50 ticks: +0.7M); both cost a little on Prosperity, so both stay off by default. Smoothing, per-side edges, a take reserve, the hysteretic mid and the join gate did not help.
- **So on the day:** run the shuffle test and `quote_markouts` on the first download before trusting any row below. If the Arena's market looks like Prosperity's, these rows stand as priors; if it looks like ABIDES's, start from the linear fair and the gate, and from a bot that does nothing as the benchmark to beat.

## On the Arena (7 Nov on)

Measured on the organisers' own simulator (the root `third_party/qminers-starter/simulator/`, or its Rust port `rust/arena_sim`) on their tapes: the playground's
`data/sample.csv.gz` and ABIDES hours written in its format. The five families in `python/bots/qminers/families/`, the
template `ArenaBot` and their grids. These are the Arena's engine and data, not a stand-in; re-measure each on the round's tape.

| Idea | Result on the Arena's data | Verdict here | Re-test on the Arena |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ladder intensity and cancellations depend on observable book state** (`gen_ladder_mechanics`) | 2026-11-08, real r3, fit first 3,000 s and test second 3,000 s. Reproduce with the command in `docs/GENERATOR_PLAN.md`, "Activity and cancellation holdout". Strictly prior 1/5/30-s book activity predicts held-out batch rate **4.200/s** vs actual **4.210/s** (constant training rate 3.659/s); log-score gain **1.980 nats/s**, 300-s block CI **[1.662,2.303]**; residual dispersion **8.627 -> 4.076**, so Poisson remains incomplete. Log-size MSE **.4647 -> .2301**. Risk starts at recognition and ends at full fill/cancel/tape end: 56,062 cancel events, 12,455 fill censors, 6 end censors, 4 orders ending before recognition. Size + CURRENT offset beats size + age by **1.506 nats per held-out cancel**, CI **[1.258,1.762]**; initial offset does not. Offset8+ hazards train/test: small **45.51/45.16 per s**, large **43.47/44.47**, versus offset2-4 small **.044/.059**, large **.019/.022**. Adding activity/age to current offset does not improve held-out score. | `measured`: observable activity and current book distance explain substantial structure beyond unconditional batch marks; these are cause-specific predictive hazards in an identified subset, not recovery of the original policy | Preserve activity-dependent arrivals/marks and dynamic cancellation exposure in the next generator experiment; model residual clustering and the still-unidentified singleton population separately |

R3 causal clock exclusion drops39,426 observations: raw-gap corr **.32171 -> .32274 / .26900 -> .26967**; gap-only MSE **7.48517 -> 7.47931 / 21.19443 -> 21.18643**, paired CI **[-.01664,.00514] / [-.04006,.02398]**, both crossing zero. R2 effect essentially zero. Broad band removal worsens MSE **+.1872/+.4615** on r3 and **+.1634/+.1261** on r2. | **Crossing the limit on purpose: a burst taker after big sweeps** (`python/bots/qminers/families/burst_taker.py`, family 13: the penalty is flat, 10,000 a second for any |position| above 100 whatever its size, orders rejected only past 1,000; so after an aggressive order of `TRIGGER` lots, an IOC of `BURST` lots its way, closed with an IOC `HOLD_MS` later) | 2026-11-07, round1.csv, `uv run python -m arena.qgrid python/bots/qminers/families/burst_taker.py data/arena/round1/round1.csv --grid TRIGGER=400,800,1500 --grid BURST=100,400,800 --grid HOLD_MS=50,200,1000 --holdout 0.25`: **every cell loses**. Within the limit (BURST 100, no penalty) the best is -28,535 (TRIGGER 1500, HOLD 1000): the move after a big sweep (~0.9 ticks within 1 s for 800+ lots) doesn't pay the spread crossed twice (~1 tick). Over the limit the penalty comes on top: best -534,648 (1500/400/50 ms, penalty 283,695), worst -46.2M (penalty 43.5M). With the flat penalty (~10 a millisecond over) any over-limit plan needs a move larger than the spread within milliseconds; round 1 has none. <br>- **The maker with more room** (2026-11-07 15:55, R1-F8 with `LIMIT` raised, `uv run python -m arena.qgrid python/bots/qminers/candidates/round1/r1_F8_take_join.py data/arena/round1/round1.csv --grid LIMIT=100,110,125,150,200`): pnl before the penalty rises 519k / 553k / 597k / 645k / 667k, but with no way back the position sits over 100 for 1,900-3,500 s (penalty 19.0M-34.9M). With an IOC back to `UNWIND_TO` the moment it passes 100 (`--set UNWIND=100 --grid LIMIT=110,125,150 --grid UNWIND_TO=60,90,100`): best 239,319 (110/100, penalty 290,417); every cell under F8, since each breach costs at least 50 (5 ms over) plus the crossed spread, and breaches come in the thousands. The room is worth something only if it costs no penalty. | `dropped here` | Only if a round's tape shows moves after a trade well beyond the spread within ~50 ms (round_start's markout at short horizons) |
| **Ladder maker: rest orders on `LEVELS` levels a side, keep each where it is when the touch moves away, and quote all the room the limit gives** (`python/bots/qminers/families/ladder_maker.py`, family 6; candidate F3, `python/bots/qminers/candidates/round0/r0_F3_full_room.py`) | 2026-11-07, playground sample_v2.csv (2M rows, 5,750 s, no momentum after trades), `uv run python -m arena.qgrid python/bots/qminers/families/ladder_maker.py data/arena/playground/sample_v2.csv --set SESSION_S=5750 --holdout 0.25 --jobs 12 --sort holdout` plus the axes below (F2, `candidates/round0/r0_F2_maker_v2.py`, as the bar: +212,831, last 25% +48,200, 6,187 fills, \|inv\| q99.7 100). Results as full / last 25%: <br>- **The ladder loses; queue places behind the touch are not worth their limit room.** `--grid LEVELS=1,2,3 --grid KEEP_DEPTH=1,3,6,20 --grid SIZE_TOUCH=50,100` (`SIZE_DEEP=50`): best `LEVELS=1, KEEP_DEPTH=1, SIZE_TOUCH=100` (F2 without its inside-the-spread rule) +212,416 / +48,783; `KEEP_DEPTH=3` +178k / +43.7k at best, `6` +136k, `20` +61k; `LEVELS=2` or `3` with `KEEP_DEPTH=1` +196k / +211k. `KEEP_DEPTH=2` with the full room below: +220,819 / +51,151 against +263,630 <br>- **Queue filters lose: every cancel gives up the queue.** `--grid IMB=0,0.2,0.4,0.7 --grid MIN_QUEUE=0,200,500` (quote a side only while its touch holds IMB times the other's lots, or MIN_QUEUE lots): off is best, 0.2 +208,775, `MIN_QUEUE=200` +191,644, 0.7 / 500 +73,443 <br>- **Quoting one tick inside on the reducing side loses.** `--grid INSIDE_POS=20,50,80,1000 --grid SKEW=60,80,100`: off is best (`SKEW=100` +213,089 / +49,124); `INSIDE_POS=80` +179k (445k lots), `20` +131k (532k lots): the extra fills, alone in front of the queue, lose <br>- **Over the limit with an IOC back loses to the penalty.** `--set SIZE_TOUCH=150 --set UNWIND=100 --grid LIMIT=100,120,150 --grid UNWIND_TO=60,80,100 --grid SKEW=80,100,130`: every `LIMIT` over 100 pays 64k-178k in penalty, +24k to +156k. An IOC unwind inside the limit (`--grid UNWIND=60,80,95 --grid UNWIND_TO=0,30,50`) pays the spread and +18k to +45k of penalty (the IOC is not counted against the other side's room): +130,738 at best, -123,661 at worst <br>- **The win: quote all the room the limit gives.** The same LIMIT 100 cell with `SIZE_TOUCH=150` +219,165 / +52,108: the reducing side quotes up to LIMIT + \|position\|, not a fixed 100. Then `--grid SIZE_TOUCH=100,150,200 --grid TOPUP=0,10,30 --grid SKEW=80,100` (`TOPUP`: add an order behind ours at the touch once that much room is free, so the side's exposure is always the full room): **`SIZE_TOUCH=200, TOPUP=10, SKEW=100` +263,282 / +61,717**, 7,764 fills, 521,952 lots, \|inv\| q99.7 100, no penalty; `SIZE_TOUCH=150` +252,984 / +58,738; `TOPUP=0` +221,596. `--grid TOPUP=1,5,10 --grid KEEP_DEPTH=1,2 --grid WIND_DOWN_S=0,60`: `TOPUP` flat (+263.6k), `WIND_DOWN_S=0` +322. `CANCEL_ALONE=0` (keep an order left in front of the touch) +242,542; `LIMIT=96` +257,246 <br>- **`run_review` (`uv run python -m experiments.rounds.run_review DIR` on an `exchange-simulator` full-logs run):** F2 9,932 maker fills, edge +0.48 ticks a lot, the mid our way after +0.06 / +0.06 / +0.08 at 1 / 5 / 30 s; F3 12,976 maker fills, edge +0.47, +0.01 / +0.02 / +0.03: no adverse selection, so the PnL is the spread times the volume and the volume is set by the limit. F3 sits at the limit: \|inv\| median 100 (F2 56), flipping between +100 and -100; it ended at -100 <br>- **On the ABIDES tapes** (`--set SESSION_S=3600`, seeds 1 and 2): F2 -51,911, F3 -55,310: both lose where trades are informed | `measured` | Round 1: run F3 and F2 and `run_review` on the first download; F3 wins only while the maker markouts stay at or above zero (it holds \|inv\| 100 most of the time, so a trend costs it the most). Price-improving inserts 15,677 (7,854 bids, 7,823 asks), lots median 14-15, mean 67-76, p90 145, p99 906; the mid 1 s after +0.42 / +0.43 in the improver's direction (the insert's own half-tick shift, then a 0.08 give-back; +0.42 / +0.45 at 5 s). Order-flow imbalance over the last 300 ms at the touch (dBid - dAsk lots), quintiles: -2,963 / -806 / -3 / +791 / +2,995 lots, the mid at 1 s -0.23 / -0.13 / 0.00 / +0.14 / +0.24 (5 s the same). every G order has a cancel row (all but 66 of 2.16M, filled or not), lifetimes the same by offset and size (median 0.34 s); IOC orders Poisson (8.0/s, gaps CV 1.00), the side independent of the last (P(same) 0.501) and of the past 2 s move, sizes independent of the touch, priced at the touch (52%) or one through (46%). **A latent price**: at a 1-tick spread a bid lands at the touch 80% of the time when the mid is up 0.5 s later and 23% when down (asks mirrored), while the PAST move changes nothing; the mid 5 s after an IOC order rises with its size, +0.10 ticks (10 lots or fewer) to +0.90 (1,000+). <br>- **The model**: a real-valued latent mid moved only by the IOC orders (each by its size bin's mean 5 s impact), bids at floor(latent) - k and asks at floor(latent) + 1 + k (k from the 1-tick offsets: 0 55%, 1 39%, 2 6%), clamped off the other touch; on a latent move the orders left on the wrong side cancel within Exp(0.2 s) (`stale_s`, calibrated on the mid's changes a second and its move after a 1-tick move). <br>- **Variants dropped** (3 seeds each, F3/F5/F6/F7 against real 426/456/483/497k): pure zero-intelligence offsets from the touch: no momentum (+0.01 at 5 s against +0.22), mid 0.81 changes/s (2.57); touch 933 lots (1,065, -12%), within 5 ticks 2,311 (2,545); 7.89 IOC orders/s (7.89), size median 57 / p90 327 (60 / 345); 2.56 mid changes/s (2.57); the mid after a trade +0.19 at 1 s, +0.20 at 5 s (+0.22 / +0.22), after one over the median +0.27 / +0.29 (+0.32 / +0.32), after a 1-tick move +0.05 at 5 s (+0.06). **PnL, real against the generated mean over 30 seeds (sd, 5th percentile, ratio)**: F3 425,553 / 480,799 (8,746, 465,391, 1.13); | **Inventory as a bet on R1-F8: online forecasts of the mid 0.5-30 s ahead, and an inventory target steered by them (quotes blocked or shrunk on the side moving away from the target, an IOC toward it)** (`python/experiments/maker/inv_bet.py`; `python/bots/qminers/candidates/round1/r1_G3_steer.py`, R1-G3: R1-F8 plus SIG = 0.383 imb1 - 0.213 imb5 + 0.035 flow(0.5 s)/100, a target clip(TGT_K SIG, +-TGT_MAX), BAND, STEER_CANCEL, TAKE_EDGE / TAKE_SIG, SKEW_DEV; every default gives F8's +519,027) | 2026-11-07, round1.csv (12,000 s, 0.1 s samples; fit on the first 75%, checked on the last 25%). **Predictors** (`uv run python -m experiments.maker.inv_bet data/arena/round1/round1.csv --trades logs/r1_F8_full/trades.csv`): the touch imbalance is the one strong feature, corr with the move in / out +0.34/+0.34 at 0.5 s, +0.24/+0.25 at 1 s, +0.11/+0.10 at 5 s, +0.05/+0.04 at 30 s; flow over 0.5 s +0.21/+0.21 at 1 s; touch inserts +0.07/+0.08, cancels -0.03/-0.04 at 1 s; the OLS on imb1, imb5, flow 0.5 s, flow 5 s: corr in / out 0.32/0.33 at 1 s, 0.14/0.14 at 5 s, 0.06/0.05 at 30 s. The forecast's move is made within a second and kept: forecast 0.25-0.5 moves +0.40 (out +0.39) at 1 s, +0.37 (+0.32) at 5 s, +0.40 (+0.32) at 10 s; above 0.5: +0.56 / +0.68 / +0.61. No cell beats F8's 386,964 in the first 75%; R1-G3 stays at its defaults (= F8) and is not a candidate. | `dropped here`: the forecast is real (1 s) but F8's depletion guard, ALLOC_K and take already trade on it; steering the inventory costs fills worth more than the lean | Each new tape: `experiments.maker.inv_bet` (does the forecast's move still stay; does corr(position, move ahead) at 5-30 s turn positive, which would make a lean worth trying again), and `--grid TGT_K=0,100 --grid BAND=60,1000` on R1-G3 |

| **The tape generator's structure: a latent price moved by √-impact, a calm and a volatile regime, exogenous jumps announced by widening placement** (`python/experiments/generator/gen_identify.py`; Phase 1 of `docs/GENERATOR_PLAN.md`, whose "Phase 1 findings" has the tables) | 2026-11-08 01:30, round1.csv, round 1's private rebuild, round2_public.csv and its mirror, round3_public.csv. `uv run python -m experiments.generator.gen_identify data/arena/round3/round3_public.csv --cache R3.npz --json R3.json` per tape (about 10 s once cached), then `--compare R1.json R1P.json R2.json R3.json`. <br>- **Impact**: every aggressive order moves the latent (the mean of the new bid and ask inserts' prices) by c·√(traded lots), permanent from 0.1 s to 300 s, with no random part on r1 (the jump's variance equals the estimator's noise). c 0.024 on r1; in the calm / volatile state 0.028 / 0.046 on r2 and 0.033 / 0.058 on r3. <br>- **Exogenous moves**: none on r1 (0.06 ticks² at 1 s, flat to 300 s). On r2 / r3: 14 / 13.6 ticks² a second, against 1.1 / 4.4 from the impacts; 97 / 96% of the 1-s residual's variance is in steps of 2+ ticks (kurtosis 427 / 423). <br>- **Jumps** (3+ ticks in 0.2 s): r2 313 (52 a 1,000 s), r3 389 (65); \|size\| median 5.6 / 5.2, q99 63 / 64; up share 0.45 / 0.51; permanent; gaps' CV 2.8 / 2.6; sign independent of the previous one's (0.48). <br>- **Regimes** (a Gaussian mixture on log 60-s variance, BIC): r1 one state; r2 / r3 calm 47 / 32% and volatile 53 / 68% of blocks, runs of 2-3.5 minutes. The volatile state brings F orders 10/s against 8, insert width 2.8-3.8 against 2.1-2.4, the 1-tick share 75% against 93%. <br>- **Placement**: offsets from the latent: r1 mean 1.03, sd 0.56; r3 volatile 1.95 / 1.71. Sizes independent of the offset. <br>- **Cancels**: every order has a cancel row, median lifetime 0.31-0.35 s; the stale hazard is 1.5-2.4x the fresh one on every tape. <br>- **Aggressive flow**: side a coin flip, uninformed (corr with the next exogenous move 0.007 or less on r2 / r3); sizes the same on every tape (median 60, p99 about 1,540, Hill α 1.7); Poisson on r1, clustered on r2 / r3 (10-s dispersion 5.5-5.9); limit priced from the latent (1.0-1.3 ticks beyond it), not from the touch. <br>- **Precursor**: for the first jump after 30 s without one, the spread goes from 1.0-1.3 to 2.2-2.7 and the insert width from 2.1-2.9 to 4.0-4.7, about 2 s before the onset. P(jump within 5 s \| spread 3+) 0.83 / 0.77 (lift 20 / 11). Insert-width episodes over q90 end in a jump within 5 s 90 / 71% of the time, lead median 1.6 / 2.75 s. <br>- **Direction**: nothing before the onset predicts it (\|corr\| ≤ 0.13). After it: a trade at a 3+ spread has the next move's side 64-66% of the time (73-76% for 5+ ticks). 5 ms after the first one, stale lots beyond the new latent are worth 1.21M / 344k ticks·lots (38k / 17k capped at 100 lots). **The race** (jumps of 5+ ticks): the first trade the jump's way comes a median 0.065 s after the reveal (the first order priced at the new level). 5 ms after that trade the stale side still holds 1,319 / 1,775 lots worth 1.54M / 2.61M ticks·lots (98k / 132k capped at 100 lots); 100 ms after, 0.65M / 1.44M. So the book drains slowly. The trigger's false positives are not costed. Aggressive counts are Poisson within the calm state (10-s dispersion 1.0-1.5) and burst within the volatile one (6.3-7.7) | `measured`: one generator, two regimes. Round 1 is the calm state throughout; rounds 2 and 3 are 53 / 68% volatile, with jumps (exogenous, permanent, clustered, random in sign) that the placement's widening announces about 2 s ahead. What moves between tapes is chiefly the volatile share, then c and the jump rate | Each new tape: run `gen_identify` and `--compare` first (the volatile share, c, jumps a 1,000 s, the precursor's hit rate). Then Phase 2 (tape_gen v2 with the regimes, jumps and pre-jump widening) and a bot rule: pull quotes when the spread or insert width crosses its q90-q97, and take the stale side once the first trade at a wide spread shows the direction |

| **A filter of the generator's hidden fair price as three extra features of R2-L9's ridge (R3-F1): new market orders land around a latent price the mid only follows, so a Bayesian filter of that price from where they land should beat the hand-made features** (`python/experiments/filter/latent_filter.py`, offline; `python/bots/qminers/candidates/round3/r3_F1_latent_filter.py`, `docs/GENERATOR_PLAN.md` Phase 3.2) | 2026-11-08 00:57-01:25. The filter: a hidden-Markov filter over the latent reference bid lb (17 integer states around the bid). Bids land at lb - k and asks at lb + 1 + k with k 0 / 1 / 2 at 55 / 39 / 6% (round 1's offsets), clamped one tick off the other touch, with a likelihood floor of 0.01. lb random-walks at 2 steps/s each way, and a market taker's filled lots move it by 3x tape_gen's impact table (round 1's 5-s mid move by size). The signal is gap = E[lb] + 0.5 - mid. <br>- **Offline** (`uv run python -m experiments.filter.latent_filter TAPE --set impact_scale=3`, 50-ms samples): the correlation of gap with the mid's move 0.1 / 0.4 / 1 / 5 s ahead, first 75% \| last 25%, with the touch imbalance's at 0.4 s in brackets: round1 (impact x1) 0.36 / 0.51 / 0.40 / 0.18 \| 0.35 / 0.50 / 0.39 / 0.18 (0.36 \| 0.36), the rebuilt private tape the same; round2_public 0.29 / 0.26 / 0.18 / 0.10 \| 0.11 / 0.15 / 0.09 / 0.05 (0.06 \| 0.05), its 5-s slope 0.98 (unbiased in ticks); hi_vol_fast1.25 (round 2's stitched tape) 0.23 / 0.20 / 0.13 / 0.06 \| 0.16 / 0.16 / 0.10 / 0.05 (0.03 \| 0.03); round3_public 0.24 / 0.31 / 0.27 / 0.11 \| 0.33 / 0.41 / 0.35 / 0.15 (0.09 \| 0.12). The impact scale's best is x1 on round 1, x3-5 on round 2 and x3 on round 3. Phase 1's sqrt rule (0.0365 sqrt(lots), filled lots or the order's size) is between x1 and x3 on round 3. Rate 0.5-8/s, eps 0.003-0.03, W 8-12 and the k weights change the correlations by under 0.01; W 4 and eps 0.1 lose. <br>- **Big moves:** on round2_public, when |gap| >= 2 ticks (3.1% of the time) the next 1 s goes the gap's way 74% of the time, mean +2.8 ticks; 5+ ticks right 28%, wrong 8%. Over 5 s: 62%, +3.4, 37% / 23%. Of all 5+ tick 1-s moves the filter called 27% at their start (|gap| >= 1 the right way) and was against 13%. On round3_public, |gap| >= 2 (10% of the time): 78%, +3.3 ticks, 37% / 5%. Round 2's jumps are 500-1,000+ lot sweeps through 5-9 levels, after inserts widen to 3-5 ticks behind both touches. <br>- **Against the F limits** (Phase 1: an F's limit is the latent mid +-1) on round 3: corr 0.32, slope 0.58; the implied latent's sd about the mid is 2.9 ticks and the filter's error sd 2.8. | **Beyond F5/X1R3: executable liquidity and action advantage** (`docs/ROUND4_RESEARCH_REVIEW.md`, `python/experiments/taker/take_completion_probe.py`) | 2026-11-08, review of main through b48c16d and real round4_public, existing F5 full logs. `uv run python -m experiments.taker.take_completion_probe data/arena/round4/py_f5_full --out data/arena/gen/r4_research/take_completion_f5.json`: 4,869 IOCs, 693,482 requested lots, 546,069 filled, **147,413 unfilled (21.26%)**; 1,345 partial, 50 zero, 3,474 full. Another 92 aggressive lots are non-IOC. For 233 incomplete orders the immediately next IOC is same-side within 100 ms: 24,220 missing lots, **85.84% next limits worse**, median one tick. Repeated requests and hindsight prices are descriptive, not recoverable PnL. Three proposed tests: (1) signal-dependent price limits / confirmed partial-fill completion, distinguished from the failed blanket FT_THRU=1; (2) action labels relative to baseline continuation with actual fills and displaced inventory, distinguished from R4-PG standalone markouts; (3) bounded audit of filter surprise / boundary mass / fresh two-sided evidence before an observation-driven change-point proposal. | **R4-A1: a restricted-action oracle on R3-F5, valued against F5's own continuation: at F5's own decisions one forced action (WAIT: F5's take skipped, its 50-ms cooldown started; TAKE_NOW: the forecast's way though under the bar; REDUCE: an IOC to flat, never past, also at F5's flips, so the flip stops at flat; COMPLETE: a partly filled take's rest at the next book), then F5 unchanged to the tape's end, by exact Rust branches (R4-PG valued a trade on its own and missed F5 making the same trade moments later)** (Rust `ridge_maker` knobs `FA_T`, `FA_ACT`, env `FA_LOG`; the WAIT rule `FT_CONFIRM`, `FT_CONFIRM_MS`, `FT_CONFIRM_FLIP`, `FT_CONFIRM_QS_LO`, `FT_CONFIRM_QS_HI`, `FT_CONFIRM_MARGIN`; all off by default: F5 1029443.00 on round4_public unchanged, with FA_LOG on too; `python/experiments/taker/action_oracle.py`) | 2026-11-08 09:05-10:30, round4_public (r4). `uv run python -m experiments.taker.action_oracle log data/arena/round4/round4_public.csv --out DIR` (4,869 takes, 3,536 of them flips, 1,345 partly filled; 19,425 near-bar decisions logged 100 ms apart), `sample DIR` (seed 4: wait 260, flip 110, take 170, reduce 110, complete 130; one per equal time stratum), then `sample DIR --seed 5 --append --n wait=160,flip=80,take=80,complete=40` and `run ... --limit 175`, `run data/arena/round4/round4_public.csv DIR --threads 6`, `report DIR --compact speed,pos,margin`: 955 branches, ~3.5 s each on 6 threads. **A300** = the branch's PnL change over [t, t+300 s] minus F5's (A5 is within a few units of it: the effect settles within 5 s). The PnL to the tape's end carries a common offset: after any perturbation the ridge's state differs for the rest of the tape, and A(end) - A300 is -111 on average (sd 265) for every action alike, so F5's path sits ~110 above its perturbed neighbours on r4. <br>- **Per action, A300** (mean, t, median, share exactly 0, share + / -; the hindsight sum of the positive part scaled to the pool on r4; mean A to the end): WAIT (340) -22.9, t -1.85, 0, 41%, 26% / 34%, 155k, -137.5; flip stopped at flat (149) +3.5, t +0.37, 0, 61%, 16% / 23%, 89k, -84.5; TAKE_NOW (209) -20.9, t -1.84, 0, 47%, 24% / 29%, 200k, -130.3; REDUCE near the bar while holding (110) -70.4, t -4.76, -36, 26%, 15% / 58%, 234k, -183.6; COMPLETE (147) -211.9 (-72.0 trimmed 5%; one -20,787 branch: 191 lots sold at once into a +127-tick move), t -1.49, -34, 18%, 22% / 59%, 41k, -337.7. Exactly 0 means F5 made the same trade 50-150 ms later at the same price. <br>- **By state** (QS_RATE terciles 1.76 / 6.58; every sampled decision at a 1-tick spread): WAIT slow / mid / fast -0.3 / -8.0 / -62.9, without / within the jump hold +2.8 / -63.3 (t -2.16); flip stopped at flat by margin 0-0.25 / 0.25-0.75 / 0.75+ +20.4 (t 1.63; blocks +14, +24, +35, +42, +22, -5) / -15.8 / -66.4, mid speed +27.3 (t 1.04); TAKE_NOW at abs(position) 100 -37.9; REDUCE with the forecast with / against the position -93.7 / -20.7; COMPLETE mid / slow -90.9 / -120.6 (t -4.9, -5.0). Per 1,000-s block: WAIT -56, +11, -20, -28, +2, -47; flip +9, +2, +4, +22, +3, -18; TAKE_NOW -14, -9, -48, -12, -11, -34; REDUCE -65, -50, -21, -75, -78, -132; COMPLETE -55, -49, -26, -56, -165, -881. <br>- **The rules, full tape** (`uv run python -m experiments.rounds.paired_rust data/arena/round4/round4_public.csv --bot F5=F5: --bot C=F5:'--set FT_CONFIRM=1' --segment 1000`): FT_CONFIRM 1 (WAIT at every take: sent only if the signal still clears the bar at the next decision) 895,459, **-133,984** (0/6 segments; the oracle's mean predicted -25.9 x 4,869 = -126k from the first 780 branches); with FT_CONFIRM_FLIP 1 (flips only) -121,281 (0/6; predicted -34.4 x 3,536 = -122k). The best cell as a rule, R3-F5's existing FT_FLIP_M (a flip stops at flat when its margin is under M): 0.1 -7,900 (-3,866 / -4,034), 0.25 -17,499, 0.4 -29,434 (3/6, 3/6, 1/6 segments). <br>- **Measurement note (8 Nov 11:30)**: `report --compact`'s "scaled to the pool" column (`action_oracle.py`, `sp * pool / n`) weights every sampled branch equally, but `stratified` draws one branch per equal time stratum and the strata hold different numbers of decisions, so each branch should count its stratum's population.
